  Interpretable Transformers often perform well in domain specific settings but lose effectiveness beyond that. Achieving ViT-like broad applicability requires models with consistent performance across domains. Recent works on Interpretable Transformers, INTR and Prompt-CAM, were developed keeping domain adaptability in mind. Prompt-CAM leverages learnable prompts to promote adaptability. However, these prompts are fixed in nature and therefore struggle to adapt to domains with substantial variation in input image characteristics, resulting in reduced interpretability. To address this limitation, we propose INTR++, which extends Prompt-CAM by incorporating a lightweight Prompt Adapter Module (PAM). PAM enables prompts to be adaptive by enriching them with domain-specific knowledge, thereby ensuring consistent interpretability across diverse domains including those with varying image characteristics such as medical imaging, micro-scopic imaging, and remote sensing imagery. INTR++ retains the standard architecture and end-to-end learning of Interpretable Transformer based models, while adding only a small number of additional parameters compared to Prompt-CAM. We demonstrate that INTR++ delivers consistent improvements in all aspects across domains like animals, plants, medical imaging, microscopic imaging, remote sensing imagery and object-centric data.
  </details>

- **[GCSR-OCC: Geometry Calibration and Semantic Routing for Open-Vocabulary Occupancy Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37149)**  `arXiv:2610.37149`  
  _Bohua Wang, Congyang Zhao, Tianxiong Lv, Chenxu Zhao, He Zheng, Hangqi Fan, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Open-vocabulary 3D occupancy prediction represents scene geometry and semantics in a unified voxel grid, enabling recognition beyond predefined categories. One approach reconstructs scenes with 3D Gaussians and converts them into voxel occupancy. However, Gaussian scale inflation and excessive overlap can distort voxel occupancy predictions, while semantic mixing can arise within Gaussians spanning multiple categories and during 3D--2D semantic feature alignment. We propose GCSR-OCC, which combines geometry calibration and multi-mode query-conditioned semantic routing for indoor occupancy prediction from a single RGB image. The Evidence-Conserving Gaussian-to-Occupancy operator (EC-G2O) uses kernel mass normalization, $p$-norm aggregation, and a learnable gain to suppress scale inflation and excessive local evidence accumulation while calibrating occupancy responses. Multi-mode Query-First Gaussian Semantic Routing (MM-QGSR) matches multiple semantic modes per Gaussian to text queries and aggregates query-specific evidence into voxels through a learnable text-conditioned router. Fine-grained subclass queries and smoothed semantic aggregation further refine voxel-level predictions. Without 3D semantic annotations, GCSR-OCC achieves state-of-the-art performance in open-vocabulary occupancy prediction on Occ-ScanNet, with 63.31% geometric IoU and 27.44% semantic mIoU. It also reduces geometric aggregation latency from 19.76 ms to 12.88 ms. Our code will be made publicly available.
  </details>

- **[MixSIS: Mixed-supervised instance segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37138)**  `arXiv:2610.37138`  
  _Christian Wilms, Emre Gülsoylu, Sven Schreiber, Simone Frintrop_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Weakly-supervised instance segmentation (WSIS) based on either box-, point-, or image-level annotations has been proposed to overcome the labor-intensive annotation process in fully-supervised instance segmentation. Despite decent results, most WSIS methods only focus on a single annotation level, ignoring that some objects are easy to spot from point annotations, while others require box or even mask annotations. Therefore, we propose the Mixed-Supervised Instance Segmentation (MixSIS) task, which allows a flexible mix of annotation levels (image, point, box, and mask) within a dataset. MixSIS has a high practical relevance, as it allows optimizing the annotation process by annotating challenging objects in more detail, while saving time on simpler instances with less detailed annotations. To address MixSIS, we propose AnnotationsMixer (AnnsMixer) as a baseline. AnnsMixer is a truly mixed-supervised instance segmentation system, which generates pseudo mask annotations from all annotation levels, even mixed within a single image. We evaluate AnnsMixer on both WSIS and MixSIS tasks, largely outperforming state-of-the-art methods. In three case studies, we also demonstrate how the unique features of MixSIS and AnnsMixer can substantially reduce the annotation time (-90%) or improve the results (+34%) in instance segmentation with a simple annotation rule or leveraging heterogeneous data. Our code is available atthis https URL
  </details>

- **[Physics Guided Diffusion Model Based Exposure Interpolation for High Dynamic Range Imaging](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37135)**  `arXiv:2610.37135`  
  _Zhengguo Li, Chaobing Zheng_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Ghosting artifacts are an open problem for popular multi-shot based high dynamic range (HDR) imaging. An effective solution is to capture two large-exposure-ratio (LER) standard dynamic range (SDR) images simultaneously via a single-shot. Brightness order reversal (BOR) artifacts are generated when the two LER images are directly fused together. It is crucial to interpolate an image with an exposure time in between the exposure times of two LER images. In this paper, a novel physics-guided diffusion model based exposure interpolation algorithm is proposed to address the BOR artifacts. An initial image is interpolated by a physics-driven component, and the residual high-frequency details are generated by a new diffusion model based component. The new diffusion model is based on the correlation analysis of generative models and includes a new predicted target to reduce the variation of the Pearson correlation between the noisy data and the target, as well as the amplification factor of the fitting error. Compared with existing neural augmented exposure interpolation algorithms on top of convolution neural networks, the proposed exposure interpolation algorithm has an inherent advantage to generate more high frequency information. Experiment results validate the proposed exposure interpolation algorithm.
  </details>

- **[MRI-to-CT Translation of Spinal Images via Anatomy-Guided Pseudo-Pairing and Adversarial Diffusion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37134)**  `arXiv:2610.37134`  
  _Eduardo Nieto, Ibai Genua, Davide Scorza, Karen López-Linares_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Magnetic Resonance Imaging (MRI) is routinely used to diagnose spinal disorders, but surgical planning often requires Computed Tomography (CT) for better visualization of vertebral bone anatomy. Synthesizing CT from MRI could avoid additional radiation exposure and streamline preoperative assessment.We propose a three-stage framework for spine MRI-to-CT synthesis. First, an anatomy-guided vertebral matching system identifies similar sagittal MRI and CT slices using spine segmentations and aligns them through rigid registration, creating pseudo-paired data from unpaired scans. Second, these pairs train an adversarial diffusion model for MRI-to-CT translation. Third, synthesized slices are assembled into 3D volumes and refined by a network that improves inter-slice continuity and structural coherence.The framework was trained on over 10,000 unpaired sagittal MRI and CT slices and evaluated on 1,911 slices from 83 MRI volumes spanning all spinal regions. Assessment combined distribution-based measures with vertebral segmentation metrics. Compared with diffusion alone, the proposed approach increased binary 3D vertebral Dice from 0.666 to 0.739. Volumetric post-processing reduced visible discontinuities between stacked slices and improved vertebral coherence in axial and coronal views.Combining anatomy-guided pseudo-pairing, adversarial diffusion and 3D refinement improves spine MRI-to-CT synthesis. Vertebral matching primarily enhanced anatomical alignment and fidelity, while volumetric refinement improved 3D consistency. These findings support further development of MRI-derived CT-like visualization for preoperative planning, particularly to preserve fine intensity details while maintaining volumetric coherence.
  </details>

- **[Multibranch Ensemble Head in Vision Foundation Models for Efficient Cucumber Phenological Stage Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37118)**  `arXiv:2610.37118`  
  _Kostiantyn Khabarlak_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Vision Foundation Models (VFMs) pretrained with self-supervised learning, such as DINOv2, provide highly transferable features, yet adapting them to small, domain-shifted datasets is still challenging. We propose a regularizing multibranch ensemble head: a training and inference scheme that pairs low- and high-capacity branches, samples a single branch stochastically at each training step, and averages both branches in probability space at inference. The design adds negligible parameters and essentially no inference overhead over a single heavy head, while improving the accuracy-efficiency trade-off under data-scarce conditions. Accurate classification of plant growth stages is critical for precision agriculture, enabling timely decisions on fertilization, irrigation, and harvesting. Therefore, as a challenging practical case we use cucumber phenological stage classification, in which flowers and fruits are frequently occluded by foliage, and stage transitions manifest primarily as subtle changes in leaf morphology. The NALEF Food Safety dataset contains images of five cucumber growth stages captured under diverse real-world conditions. On it we evaluate a broad range of backbones, including DINOv2 and DINOv3 ViT and ConvNeXt variants, against strongly supervised counterparts (ResNet, EfficientNet, ViT). The proposed DINOv2 ViT-S with multibranch head achieves a Macro F1 of 0.978 and Cohen's Kappa of 0.971, matching the performance of models more than ten times larger in parameters and GFlops, while requiring only 12.2 GFlops and 22.3M parameters, making it deployable on smartphones and edge devices for phenological stage classification.
  </details>

- **[A First-Step Bias Restores Lost Diversity in Diffusion Model Distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37110)**  `arXiv:2610.37110`  
  _Wenwu Tang, Dong Wang, Yun Cheng, Olga Saukh_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Distribution-matching distillation enables high-quality image generation in only a few denoising steps. However, distilled student models often map different initial noise samples to similar outputs under the same prompt. To locate where diversity is lost, we compare the teacher and student along the sampling trajectory. The largest measured reduction in the response to different initial noises occurs during the first, high-noise step. When we replace the first step with the teacher's corresponding trajectory and leave the final three student steps unchanged, most of the output diversity returns. This suggests that the first step is a key point for restoring diversity. Motivated by this finding, we introduce \emph{ReSeed}, a post-distillation method that adds a seed-response bias adapter only to the first student step and aligns its velocity with the teacher's first-interval secant velocity. All student weights and later denoising steps remain unchanged. We evaluate ReSeed on three text-to-image models (SD3-Medium, SD3.5-Medium, and FLUX.1-dev) and one text-to-video model (Wan2.1-T2V-1.3B). For a distilled 4-step FLUX.1-dev model, \emph{ReSeed} requires only $0.00196\%$ additional trainable parameters relative to the base model and is trained on 8,000 prompt--noise pairs, while keeping the distilled student fully frozen and requiring no target images, perceptual losses, or adversarial losses. It improves DINO diversity by $48.3\%$ while maintaining competitive visual quality. These results show that substantial diversity can be recovered through a lightweight, data-efficient tuning of only the first denoising step, without finetuning the full student model.
  </details>

- **[SplatScout: Query-Driven Part-Level Referring Segmentation in Gaussian Scenes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37098)**  `arXiv:2610.37098`  
  _Yihan Zang, Da Li, Shinkyu Park, Ivan Viola, Dominik Engel_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Existing referring segmentation methods on Gaussian scenes primarily target whole objects, relying on segmenter-generated candidates from which a vision-language model (VLM) selects. Such predetermined fixed candidates may omit fine-grained parts needed as targets or cues for identifying objects. We introduce SplatScout, a query-driven, closed-loop approach that places perception under VLM control. The model actively selects views, directs segmentation, and inspects rendered 3D results to refine its predictions. For part-level evaluation, we introduce PartRef-ScanNet++, comprising 261 manually verified queries across 12 ScanNet++ scenes with mesh-derived ground truth. On PartRef-ScanNet++, SplatScout achieves 0.538 mIoU, substantially outperforming existing methods. Ablations demonstrate the benefits of iterative refinement, while comparisons across VLM backbones highlight the importance of effective tool-feedback utilization. SplatScout also achieves higher scores on four existing referring benchmarks. Code and the PartRef-ScanNet++ benchmark are available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[TriDifNet: A Unified Three-Task Diffusion Network for End-to-End RGB-T Perception](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37097)**  `arXiv:2610.37097`  
  _Qianchao He, Jing Liu, Yunhao Li, Xiaoqing Tian_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Infrared--visible (IR--VIS) multi-modal perception supports robust scene understanding under challenging illumination. Infrared and visible images are often misaligned due to platform motion, viewpoint changes, and modality-dependent distortions, causing registration errors to propagate into subsequent fusion and detection stages. We propose TriDifNet, a unified three-task diffusion network for end-to-end RGB-T perception. To the best of our knowledge, TriDifNet is the first diffusion-based network that jointly optimizes registration, fusion, and detection for misaligned RGB-T pedestrian perception. TriDifNet consists of three diffusion-based modules, i.e., the Registration Diffusion Module (RegDM), the Fusion Diffusion Module (FusDM), and the Detection Diffusion Module (DetDM). RegDM aligns the unaligned infrared image to the optical reference by estimating a dense deformation field. FusDM performs residual conditional diffusion to generate a detection-oriented fused representation with optical structural cues and complementary infrared saliency. DetDM formulates pedestrian detection as an iterative denoising process in bounding-box space. To couple the three tasks, TriDifNet introduces a closed-loop optimization mechanism, where detection semantics guide fusion and registration, and the fusion result further regularizes geometric alignment. A stage-wise progressive training strategy and a lightweight Nash multi-task learning (Nash-MTL)-based late-stage refinement are further adopted to balance registration, fusion, and detection optimization. Extensive experiments on the KAIST multispectral pedestrian benchmark show that TriDifNet achieves superior downstream detection performance under misaligned RGB-T settings. Against strong system-level competitors, TriDifNet achieves 65.44\% Recall, 57.55\% mAP$_{50}$, and 26.83\% mAP$_{50:95}$.
  </details>

- **[Learning to Look Beyond Screens: Transferable Visual Observation from GUI Grounding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37093)**  `arXiv:2610.37093`  
  _Ruilin Yao, Xinyu Qiu, Tianyu Zou, Yi Rong, Shengwu Xiong, Shuheng Shen, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Visual perception is evolving from passive recognition toward active, task-oriented observation. We investigate GUI grounding as a scalable source of spatial supervision for learning observation behaviors that transfer beyond interfaces. We introduce LBS (Look Beyond Screens), a vision-language model trained through supervised initialization on teacher-generated GUI grounding trajectories, followed by reinforcement learning for multi-turn crop-and-inspect decisions. Under fixed inference settings, LBS models based on Qwen3.5-35B-A3B and Qwen3.8-27B consistently improve over their corresponding baselines. Gains beyond supervised fine-tuning transfer to PerceptionBench for both backbones and to HR-Bench and ChartQAPro for Qwen3.5-35B-A3B, supporting observation strategies that generalize beyond GUI-specific imitation. Further analysis suggests complementary roles for the two stages: SFT establishes visual inspection primitives, while RL improves their downstream effectiveness. These findings support GUI grounding as a practical supervision paradigm for transferable active visual perception and broader multimodal reasoning. Our code and model are publicly available atthis https URLandthis https URL, respectively.
  </details>

- **[Systematic Morphometric Brain Autoencoding for Representation Learning in Neurodegenerative Diseases](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37074)**  `arXiv:2610.37074`  
  _Nuria Balbás-Esteban, Albert Belenguer-Llorens, Elaheh Moradi, Jussi Tohka, Vanessa Gómez-Verdejo, Carlos Sevilla-Salcedo_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Early identification of neurodegenerative disorders using structural T1-weighted MRI is essential for timely clinical intervention, yet supervised deep learning models often struggle to generalize due to limited annotations and scanner-induced distribution shift. Self-supervised learning offers a promising alternative; however, existing approaches are largely adapted from natural image paradigms, relying on contrastive objectives or generic masked reconstruction with voxel-wise losses, which may not fully capture the distributed morphometric patterns associated with neurodegeneration.We introduce SYMBA (SYstematic Morphometric Brain Autoencoding), a self-supervised pretraining framework specifically designed for structural brain MRI. SYMBA integrates anatomically constrained masking, a multi-scale structural similarity (MS-SSIM) reconstruction objective, and a progressive curriculum that transitions from local anatomical detail to global brain morphology. These components explicitly incorporate neuroanatomical structure into the self-supervised objective rather than relying on generic image priors.Pretrained on 6,953 T1-weighted MRI scans from nine heterogeneous cohorts, SYMBA is evaluated using frozen linear probing on two independent unseen datasets, enabling a strict assessment of out-of-distribution generalization. Across multiple clinical tasks, SYMBA consistently outperforms recent neuroimaging foundation models, achieving 0.930 AUROC for Alzheimer's disease classification, 0.772 for mild cognitive impairment conversion prediction, and strong performance in amyloid status prediction. In low-label regimes, SYMBA demonstrates high sample efficiency, reaching approximately 0.90 AUROC for AD/CN classification using only six labeled subjects.
  </details>

- **[Learning to Code the World: Harness Evolution for Compositional 3D Reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37046)**  `arXiv:2610.37046`  
  _Haoyu Yao, Chenguo Lin, Ruize Du, Yadong Mu_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Reconstructing editable and compositional 3D scenes from a real image requires jointly reasoning about geometry, object structure, and spatial relationships. We introduce SceneEvolve, a framework that represents 3D scenes as executable code and improves reconstruction by evolving the harness around a small open-source agent. SceneEvolve combines SceneAct, a compositional semantic action interface built on parameterized primitives, hierarchical composition, and semantic spatial relations, with SceneHarness-RSI, which leverages a stronger teacher agent to iteratively evolve the harness using feedback from development-set reconstructions. Throughout harness evolution, the model parameters, action semantics, and execution backend remain fixed. The resulting harness provides a reusable reconstruction procedure that enables the small open-source model to reconstruct unseen scenes without parameter updates or teacher assistance. Across two in-domain datasets and one out-of-domain dataset, SceneEvolve with the comparatively compact Qwen3.8-27B model improves all four evaluated reconstruction metrics while reducing mean inference time by 47.0% relative to the base model. More notably, despite relying on a substantially smaller model, SceneEvolve achieves 32.3% higher reconstruction quality than the large proprietary GPT-5.6-Sol model, while reducing mean equivalent deployment cost by 59.8%. Furthermore, the harness evolved using Qwen3.8-27B also generalizes to Kimi-K3, achieving comparable reconstruction performance to GPT-6-Astra. The project page isthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[Interpretable Deep Learning Models for Breast Cancer Diagnosis Using Medical Imaging](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37026)**  `arXiv:2610.37026`  
  _Sami Alnawali, Muhammad Atif Saeed, Abdul Hadi, Azka Atiq, Akhtar Jamil, Ahmad Din_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Deep learning shows promise for breast cancer diagnosis, but limited interpretability hinders clinical adoption. We benchmark ten ImageNet-pretrained models (ResNet50, VGG19, InceptionV3, DenseNet121, Xception, MobileNetV2, EfficientNet, ConvNeXt, ViT, and Swin Transformer) for benign-versus-malignant classification on the BreakHis histopathology dataset using a patient-level split, and explain their predictions with Grad-CAM, LIME, Integrated Gradients, and LRP. DenseNet121 performed best, with 99.4% test accuracy and an AUC-ROC of 0.999, and significantly outperformed all other models (p < 0.001). Transformer-based models trailed the CNNs. The explanations consistently highlighted nuclei-rich and glandular regions of diagnostic relevance. Combining transfer learning with explainable AI can thus yield accurate, interpretable tools for breast cancer diagnosis.
  </details>

- **[Ground-to-Satellite Knowledge Distillation for Crop Classification under Temporal and Geographic Shift](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37021)**  `arXiv:2610.37021`  
  _Miro Miranda, Rocco Sedona, Valerio Marsocci, Gabriele Cavallaro, Claudia Paris_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Continuous and up-to-date crop type mapping is essential for agricultural monitoring but is often hampered by limited reference data. The growing availability of ground-level field photographs offers a promising way to address this challenge. When combined with satellite time series, these images provide complementary perspectives on the same parcel, capturing crop characteristics from distinctly different perspectives. However, field photographs are not always available for each satellite pixel at inference time, limiting their potential in scalable crop type mapping. We investigate whether ground photographs can provide privileged supervision for crop classification built on pretrained satellite embeddings. A photo teacher based on frozen CLIP features supervises classifiers trained on AlphaEarth and TESSERA representations; photographs are used only during training. On a paired field photo and satellite imagery dataset with 17 crop classes, knowledge distillation consistently improves over satellite-only classification; for frozen embeddings the margin grows under temporal shift, reaching up to +5.5 macro-F1 points, and widens in geographic holdouts (+3.7 vs +3.0 in-distribution). These results show that ground-view supervision can complement satellite embeddings and improve their robustness without changing inference requirements.
  </details>

- **[GPA-VGGT: Geometry-Preserving Acceleration of VGGT](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37000)**  `arXiv:2610.37000`  
  _Muyeol Sung, Jangwon Kim, Jongwoo Lim_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Feed-forward geometry models such as VGGT recover camera parameters, depth maps, and point maps from one to hundreds of views in a single forward pass, making them powerful backbones for multi-view 3D perception. However, VGGT's global attention is quadratic in the number of frames and dominates end-to-end runtime on long sequences, which has motivated a range of training-free efficiency methods such as token merging. These methods, however, come with a drop in accuracy that makes them hard to use in place of VGGT. We propose GPA-VGGT, a training-free acceleration method that replaces global attention in early blocks with frame attention augmented by a shared cross-frame K/V set. In the remaining blocks, we merge only keys and values while preserving all queries, restore the attention mass of the tokens each merged key represents, and randomly project the matching metric to 128 dimensions to reduce matching cost. Across diverse multi-view geometry tasks, GPA-VGGT provides training-free acceleration at speedups comparable to prior work while preserving the information required for precise geometric estimation, retaining over 99% of VGGT's pose accuracy under AUC@3.
  </details>

- **[LearnPrunerV2: Learnable Progressive Token Pruning for Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36986)**  `arXiv:2610.36986`  
  _Rinyoichi Takezoe, Yaqian Li, Weiren Wang, Zihao Bo, Huihong Shi, Anzhou Hou, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Vision-Language Models (VLMs) have demonstrated remarkable multimodal capabilities but suffer from prohibitive computational overhead due to the massive number of visual tokens. While token pruning is a promising solution, existing methods still suffer from two major drawbacks: 1) unreliable pruning criteria that fail to accurately localize task-specific regions due to inherent attention biases or query-agnostic designs, and 2) suboptimal pruning strategies that employ rigid, non-adaptive schedules, overlooking the dynamic evolution of token redundancy across different model depths. To address these challenges, we propose LearnPrunerV2, which extends the previous two-stage heuristic framework (LearnPruner) into a fully learnable architecture. Specifically, we integrate lightweight learnable modules into every layer of the VLM backbone, which estimate token importance by fusing intrinsic visual saliency with extrinsic query relevance captured via a shadow token. By uncovering a consistent staircase redundancy pattern across VLM layers, we further introduce Budget-Deterministic Pruning (BDP) to establish a hardware-friendly schedule with fixed per-layer budgets. Extensive experimental results across various benchmarks and VLMs demonstrate that LearnPrunerV2 consistently outperform previous state-of-the-art methods. Notably, LearnPrunerV2 preserves 99.8% of the original performance when pruning 77.8% of visual tokens on LLaVA-v1.5, achieving a superior accuracy-efficiency trade-off.
  </details>

- **[Explicit Regional Representations For Video Quality Assessment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36978)**  `arXiv:2610.36978`  
  _Fuyi Yang, Lei Yang, Andrew Liu, Chenyu Wei, Xiaoyi Guo, Ming Kai Hsu_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  End-to-end video quality assessment (VQA) methods leave the regional evidence underlying their predictions largely implicit. We introduce an explicit pixel-level regional representation for VQA, instantiated as a Quality-Relevance Heatmap (QRH) that combines localized degradation evidence with perceptual priors under vision-language model guidance, without requiring pixel-level annotations. We apply QRH to existing VQA models through two deterministic fragment-sampling strategies: distribution-preserving sampling approximates the original sampler's QRH-score distribution without retraining, while quality-aware sampling prioritizes regions with high QRH scores and is paired with fine-tuning. Experiments with DOVER on multiple benchmarks show that the former closely approximates predictions averaged over random samples, while the latter improves most evaluated metrics across popular VQA benchmarks. These results support explicit regional representations as a useful interface for downstream VQA.
  </details>

- **[Dense Representation Learning and Differentiable Shape Modeling for Spine Landmark Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36951)**  `arXiv:2610.36951`  
  _Augustin Daridon, Sofiane Sifaoui, Ayman Assi, Mohamad Karam, Wafa Skalli, Elsa Angelini, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Spine landmark detection from spinal X-ray is a key step for estimating spine deformation severity, for building biomechanical models, and for pre-operative planning. We reframe anatomical landmark detection as a pixel-level contrastive representation learning problem, matching dense pixel embeddings with landmark prototypes. Surprisingly, even with a generic U-Net backbone, this formulation results in more discriminative heatmaps compared to the traditional heatmap regression paradigm, leading to improved discrimination between vertebral corners on distinct vertebra. Furthermore, we combine the resulting heatmaps with learnable shape priors over the global configuration of spinal landmarks to resolve ambiguities in landmark locations, yielding a simple and practical approach to spinal landmark detection. Our approach is competitive, in terms of landmark accuracy and clinical parameter estimation, with the state-of-the-art on the public AASCE dataset and on two private spinal X-ray datasets. Our code and pre-trained weights are available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[PREEN: Exemplar-Based Color Style Transfer with Smartphone Editor Presets](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36896)**  `arXiv:2610.36896`  
  _Beomjin Ahn_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  We present PREEN (Preset Regression via an Exemplar-based Editor Network), which casts color style transfer as regression onto the 29 parameters of a deterministic image editor. Given a content image and a style reference, PREEN predicts a preset, a vector of editor slider values that is interpretable and editor-consumable, not a raster image. The key obstacle is supervision, since an arbitrary real reference lacks a preset ground truth. We therefore supervise with a style-transfer teacher, whose target looks we invert into the editor's parameter space as pseudo-presets, then pretrain a CLIP-conditioned regressor and refine it with LoRA under an image-level CIELAB loss. On the human-curated PST50 benchmark, PREEN applies the reference's color while preserving structural fidelity, leading the baselines on pixel- and perceptual-fidelity metrics. The preset is compact and predicted at sub-second on-device latency. The low-dimensional subspace also exposes a single inference-time strength knob that traces a continuous default-to-vivid trajectory in one forward pass, with every intermediate state a valid editor preset. A two-axis human study shows that raters prefer the vivid end for matching the reference but the default as a standalone photo, a trade-off the knob spans without retraining.
  </details>

- **[HoloRecon: Incremental Reconstruction of Long-Range Panoramas with Global Consistency](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36876)**  `arXiv:2610.36876`  
  _Zhuoxiao Li, Xinyi Liu, Hongye Hou, Jing OU, Kang Du, Shuai Zhang, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Long-sequence panoramic reconstruction requires detailed scene geometry and globally consistent camera poses across distant revisits. Panoramic SLAM and incremental SfM rely on feature correspondences, but repeated, similar structures and small partial overlap make reliable long-range connections difficult to establish, leaving accumulated drift unresolved. Feed-forward models offer detailed local geometry, but their high computational costs limit inference window size, necessitating additional constraints for global consistency. This motivates combining continuous feature-based tracking and sparse mapping with rapid, high-quality local reconstruction from feed-forward models to achieve accurate localization and globally consistent dense mapping over long sequences. We therefore propose a panoramic reconstruction framework that couples an ORB-based sparse panoramic mapping frontend with a fine-tuned feed-forward model. Our key insight is that predicted local geometry enables 3D verification of feature matches across distant revisits, yielding long-range camera constraints even between regions. We calibrate predicted depths with sparse landmarks and use verified connections to optimize the global pose graph, updating dense geometry with the corrected camera poses. We also introduce PanoRecon, a seven-scene benchmark with repetitive architecture, large and small loops, and reference camera poses and LiDAR geometry for evaluating global consistency. Our method achieves the lowest mean absolute trajectory error and high F-1 Score among the evaluated methods. Project Page:this https URL
  </details>

- **[RangeRectQuant: Compressing Scientific Fields for Every Future Rectangular Sum](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36873)**  `arXiv:2610.36873`  
  _Jiahao Zhang, Xinling Wen, Yu Chen_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Scientific rasters are often stored before the regions to be measured are known. Independent pixel-error bounds can force exact integer reconstruction when one absolute error budget covers every future rectangle. Coordinated changes allow every pixel to change while every rectangular sum remains accurate. A prefix sum accumulates values from the top-left corner. Four such sums determine any rectangular total. RangeRectQuant preserves the pixel range and designated exact samples while controlling errors in prefix sums. At each row position, we characterize exactly which errors let the remaining pixels meet these constraints. These errors form a single integer interval, even when many completions exist. A backward pass finds its endpoints; a forward pass uses them to choose quantized pixels. The resulting field meets the prescribed error budget for every axis-aligned rectangle, with linear work and workspace proportional to one row. Experiments on geographically held-out built-surface and water-occurrence maps show substantial storage savings over alternatives meeting the same requirements. Data producers encode once; later users measure arbitrary rectangles without the source, re-encoding, or query-specific correction.
  </details>

- **[Forget the Data, Keep the Capability: Switchable Unlearning for Retrieval](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36858)**  `arXiv:2610.36858`  
  _Jingong Chen, Kyle Gao, Guangyuan Li, Chulwoo Pack, Sijia Liu, Kwanghee Won_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Privacy compliance requires re-identification models to selectively forget individuals while preserving their ability to recognize others. For this, we present MoME, a switchable framework that can adapt pre-trained backbones for privacy compliance, allowing the same model to switch between Full mode for standard inference and Core mode for unlearned inference. MoME splits one backbone into core parameters that preserve retrieval and zero-initialized parasitic parameters that carry the full-data knowledge, and training them under gradient isolation so the core never receives a forget-set gradient. Disabling the branch yields Core, which satisfies a request to delete a person's training data, and enabling it yields Full, which retains what the deleted data taught, so honoring the request is a flag flip rather than a retraining run. We prove Core independent of the forget set up to an audited set of coupling channels and introduce the rank-based Forgetting-Rank statistic to audit that independence in a trained model. On Market-1501, VeRi-776 and RegDB, Core reads at a retrained reference under three membership attacks while retrieving above it, and the retrieval advantage carries to ResNet and CLIP backbones. Code and weights are available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[Evaluating AlphaEarth Embeddings for Crop Classification: Separability, Temporal Dynamics, and Cross-Regional Transferability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36857)**  `arXiv:2610.36857`  
  _Priyanka Goyal, Maitreyee Sar, Adway Mitra, Manjira Sinha_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Earth Observation (EO)-based agricultural monitoring is shifting from pixel-level spectral analysis toward representation-driven modeling, yet systematic evaluation of learned representations across spatial, temporal, and domain-generalization dimensions remains limited. Geospatial foundation models generate high-dimensional embeddings encoding spatio-temporal land-surface properties, but their utility for agricultural applications remains insufficiently understood. This study presents a representation-agnostic evaluation framework integrating geometric separability, temporal dynamics, spatial robustness, and cross-regional generalisation. A 64-dimensional AlphaEarth embedding space is benchmarked against Sentinel-2 reflectance, NDVI-derived phenology, and harmonic features using field-scale data from the Conterminous United States (CONUS; 2017-2023) and Northern India (2021).Traditional features achieve strong linear separability (Fisher ratio), while embeddings provide superior distribution-aware separation -- Bhattacharyya and Mahalanobis distances up to an order of magnitude higher for spectrally similar pairs (e.g. Corn/Soybeans: 3.9 vs. 0.10). Embeddings uniquely capture multi-year crop rotation dynamics, reaching a separability ratio of 1.784 versus a maximum of 1.003 across all engineered features, and exhibit lower residual spatial autocorrelation of errors (mean Moran's $I \approx 0.17$). However, harmonic and NDVI features show stronger cross-continental transferability (US $\to$ India Macro F1: 0.493 vs. 0.326 for embeddings), reflecting better distributional alignment. The combined feature space yields balanced performance, supporting hybrid modeling. These findings reveal a fundamental trade-off between representational richness and cross-region transferability, providing a structured framework for feature selection in large-scale crop classification.
  </details>

- **[uTHCD-C: A Corruption-Robustness Benchmark for Tamil Handwritten Character Recognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36850)**  `arXiv:2610.36850`  
  _Preethi S, Meeradevi T, Arun Prakash A_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Evaluation of Tamil handwritten character recognition (HCR) has largely focused on clean test images, and a standardized multi-corruption robustness benchmark for isolated Tamil HCR has not been established. We introduce uTHCD-C, a corruption-robustness benchmark based on the unconstrained Tamil Handwritten Character Database (uTHCD). The benchmark defines ten corruption types, including a handwriting-specific stroke-thinning corruption, at five severity levels. We evaluate nine convolutional and transformer-based architectures using five-fold stratified cross-validation and writer-independent testing. Robustness is quantified using mean Corruption Error (mCE), normalized against a VGG16-BN reference. We additionally compare parameter count, computational complexity, and inference throughput to characterize the computational requirements of the evaluated architectures. The results establish comparative robustness baselines for Tamil HCR, revealing substantial differences across architectures: ConvNeXt and Swin achieve the lowest mCE values (0.75 and 0.83, respectively), whereas ShuffleNet and MnasNet exhibit higher values (1.55 and 1.85, respectively). The accompanying efficiency measurements characterize the trade-offs between corruption robustness and computational requirements. By providing standardized corruption configurations, evaluation protocols, and comparative results, uTHCD-C establishes a reproducible basis for future research on robust and computationally efficient Tamil HCR.
  </details>

- **[AxisQ: Depth-Adaptive Channel--Frequency Quantization for Camouflaged Object Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36844)**  `arXiv:2610.36844`  
  _Chi Ruan, Tianqi Li, Xin He, Xue Geng, Ming-Ming Cheng, Yun Liu_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Camouflaged object detection (COD) depends on weak texture and boundary cues that can be destroyed by aggressive post-training quantization (PTQ). Existing PTQ methods mainly optimize quantizers or reconstruct network blocks, while using a single precision-allocation domain throughout hierarchical backbones. Instead, we introduce AxisQ, a depth-adaptive bit-allocation framework motivated by the observation that the useful allocation axis changes with feature depth. Under a fixed per-layer average activation-bit budget, AxisQ allocates precision in the channel domain for shallow features and in the local-frequency domain for deep features, using COD-aware sensitivity to better preserve subtle foreground regions and ambiguous boundaries. We also formulate a boundary-aware Hessian approximation for reconstruction by incorporating boundary-weighted gradient and error statistics. Experiments across multiple COD models and diverse benchmarks demonstrate improved accuracy over the strongest baselines at low bit widths, with relative weighted F-measure gains of 12.7% for CFRN and 33.3% for CamoFormer-P on CAMO under W2A2 quantization. These results support adapting the allocation domain to feature depth as a transferable quantization strategy for hierarchical COD models.
  </details>

- **[MVRCap: Towards Comprehensive Video Captioning via Hierarchical Multi-Dimensional Verifiable Rewards](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36838)**  `arXiv:2610.36838`  
  _Weihuang Lin, Xianing Chen, Zhibin Wang, Congzhi Zhang, Yiwei Ma, Jiayi Ji, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Despite significant progress, current models struggle to generate comprehensive video captions, constrained primarily by coarse-grained supervision, which often fails to capture spatial-temporal dynamics and the underlying logical intent. To address this issue, we introduce MVRCap, a novel framework that reformulates video captioning as a reinforcement learning task steered by structured verification. Central to our approach is a Fine-Grained Verifiable Reward mechanism, serving as dense supervision to enforce rigorous visual-semantic alignment. Specifically, we construct this reward system anchored by three complementary pillars: a Hierarchical Concept Coverage Reward utilizing our newly constructed MVRCap-QA dataset to ensure multi-dimensional completeness and semantic depth, a Factuality Verification Reward to enhance factual consistency, and a Conciseness Constraint to maximize semantic density. Furthermore, we curate the high-quality MVRCap-SFT dataset, providing structured annotations across six key dimensions, to serve as a robust foundational initialization for the RL policy in this training pipeline. Extensive experiments demonstrate that MVRCap achieves state-of-the-art performance on authoritative benchmarks including VDC, CAPability, and VC4VG.
  </details>

- **[Drop the Details, Get the Gist: Dynamic Conceptual Representation for Video Recognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36832)**  `arXiv:2610.36832`  
  _Ryoya Yoshiyama, Bowen Wang, Yuta Nakashima_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Concept Bottleneck Models (CBMs), which route predictions through a layer of human-interpretable concepts, have substantially improved the interpretability of visual classification by exposing an explicit, language-based rationale for each prediction. Most existing work focuses on static images, and temporal dynamics in videos have been underexplored in conceptual representation. We propose a framework that extends concept-based image recognition to procedural video understanding. It represents a multi-step video as a temporal sequence of segment-level concept score vectors and feeds this sequence into a Transformer encoder to predict the class label. We also explore a way to estimate the contribution of each concept for further interpretability. We evaluated three concept sets of different granularities on cooking and household-activity videos and found that coarser-grained concepts are sufficient for classifying these videos, which implies that fine-grained textual descriptions may be unnecessary.
  </details>

- **[Right Click, Wrong Box: Grading GUI Grounding by What a Click Does](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36831)**  `arXiv:2610.36831`  
  _Xiaokang Yuan, Binhui Yao, Luwei Yang, Ruimin Chen, Zeng Wang_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Leading GUI grounders score 93 to 96% on ScreenSpot-v2, and reported gaps between new and prior models are about a point. At this level the useful question is what the remaining errors are. Benchmarks count a click as correct only inside one annotated box, but interfaces often expose one action through several elements: a duplicated link, the label of a checkbox, the title next to a logo. The box is not wrong. The rule is incomplete. We call a click correct when executing it produces the same outcome as clicking the annotated target. A probe that executes and fingerprints about a thousand clicks per page turns this definition into outcome maps and an execution judge for live web applications, with no new annotation. We execute the predictions of three strong 7--8B grounders on a code forge and an online shop. About one in five and one in three of the clicks that box scoring rejects reach the intended outcome, and the shares remain one in seven and one in three when the instructions are rewritten as natural tasks. A blind audit of 11 models on ScreenSpot-v2 (three 7--8B grounders and eight 3B variants) finds the same effect on web screenshots, with two independent reviewers on a 60-unit subset. The share is highest for the strongest grounders. Training seeds and test-set sampling add noise that more runs and paired intervals control. Box scoring instead under-counts correct clicks, which repetition does not remove, and the audit-based correction of 0.2 to 1.8 points on ScreenSpot-v2 is as large as the gaps the field reports. The correction is needed in evaluation. In our 3B GRPO study, crediting equivalent clicks in the reward gives no stable gain over box rewards across three seeds and four test sets. We release the probe, the outcome maps, the audit and every prediction.
  </details>

- **[Visual Autoregressive Generation with Next Token Imagination](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36793)**  `arXiv:2610.36793`  
  _Ruihang Jiang, Zongyao Zhao, Zhaolin Wang, Yuanwei Liu, Xiatian Zhu_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  The space of possible visual content is enormous, and arguably unbounded. Any training set, however large, therefore covers only a tiny fraction of it. Yet generative models are typically trained to fit only the images they are given. In visual autoregressive (AR) generation, for example, each token is predicted from the preceding ground-truth (GT) tokens and supervised solely by the GT next token. Confining learning to observed data in this way limits how much of the visual distribution a model can capture. In this work, we hypothesize that letting a model imagine beyond its training data, by exploring plausible visual realizations it has never observed, can ease this limitation. We use visual AR generation as a proof of concept, since its fixed, discrete token space makes imagination easier to steer and constrain. We propose Next Token Imagination (NTI), a method that allows the model to explore beyond the GT next token, subject to a carefully designed criterion that keeps this imagination bounded by the underlying visual distribution. Extensive experiments on ImageNet show that NTI substantially and consistently improves the fidelity of generated images, approaching state-of-the-art diffusion and AR models while using a smaller model and lower training cost.
  </details>

- **[OmniGlimpse: Asymmetric Identity Memory for Online Multi-View Tracking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36785)**  `arXiv:2610.36785`  
  _Rangya Zhang, Jiaping Xiao, Yujing Shang, Yuhang Zhang, Mingsheng Li, Mir Feroskhan_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Multi-view tracking extends visual coverage beyond a single camera, providing complementary observations when targets are occluded or leave the view. This raises a question: how can observations from different views be matched to the correct identities without offline scene-specific training or camera calibration? Progress toward this goal remains limited because many offline formulations assume such deployment-specific knowledge, while online adaptation can amplify association errors. We study this problem as online multi-view identity maintenance (OMIM): targets are initialized once per view, with no further identity labels provided after each initialization. To this end, we introduce OmniGlimpse, an asymmetric propose-verify framework that separates adaptive association from stable identity consolidation. At the representation level, short-term local and cross-view evidence is mapped into target-specific adaptive spaces to propose associations, whereas long-term identity distributions are maintained for verification in a canonical space defined by a frozen encoder. At the update level, short-term evidence may guide current associations, whereas, after bootstrapping, only verified observations may update persistent memory or drive adaptation. Under a unified target-specified evaluation protocol, OmniGlimpse improves over the best evaluated online baseline for each metric by 9.1 IDF1 and 22.1 CVIDF1 points on M3Track. The corresponding gains on MvMHAT are 20.3 and 26.2 points. Despite using no offline scene-specific training, it outperforms offline-trained references in IDF1 and remains competitive in cross-view association. These results support an approach to online identity maintenance that adapts to changing appearances while consolidating reliable evidence into stable identity anchors.
  </details>

- **[CVBL-CCTV-Face Dataset for Real-World Face Enhancement and Recognition in Surveillance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36781)**  `arXiv:2610.36781`  
  _Manish Das, Ajay Kumar Yadav, Shiv Ram Dubey, Satish Kumar Singh_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  In real-world surveillance settings, facial captures from closed-circuit television systems suffer from degradations such as low resolution, motion blur, noise, and occlusions, which undermine dependable face recognition. In this study, we propose a framework for super-resolution and identity recognition, aimed at surveillance applications. For this purpose, we present the CVBL-CCTV-Face Dataset which is a dataset of real-world images taken in an unpaired manner where each pair consists of a low-quality (LQ) image of a face captured by a 8MP IP Dome CCTV camera together with a high-quality (HQ) reference image that is taken in controlled settings. In order to cope with the various problems posed by such datasets, we construct a modular system that involves face detection, deep learning-based super-resolution of low-quality face images, and identity recognition through L2-normalized embeddings extracted by an embedding model. Enhancement models are trained and tested according to three different setups, and the system is deployed for identity verification in a real-time unconstrained environment.
  </details>

- **[Evaluation-Guided Demonstration Refinement for Vision-Language-Action Policies: The Winning Entry in the MetaFine Open Competition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36765)**  `arXiv:2610.36765`  
  _Lifan Zheng, Guotao Chen, Zhenyang Li, Shujie Cui, Qiang Huang, Fan Liu_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  We fine-tuned a separate $\pi_{0.5}$ policy for each of the five tasks in the MetaFine Open Competition, with a task-specific training mixture and number of actions executed per prediction. Training started from the official demonstrations and expert procedures. Local evaluation exposed recurring grasping, pushing, and insertion failures. We addressed them by revising the demonstrated motions and by adding expert continuations from states the policy reached, while keeping nominal demonstrations in training. Checkpoints were compared on every instruction, so that a gain on one instruction could not hide a loss on another. Our submission, MAI, ranked first overall with a score of 96.66.
  </details>

- **[GeoGSplat: Geometry-Grounded Non-Pixel-Aligned Gaussian Splatting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36760)**  `arXiv:2610.36760`  
  _Jiale Wang, Chen Zhao, Haoyang Zhang, Yanhao Wu, Fei He, Yanhu Shan, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Feed-forward Gaussian Splatting enables efficient novel view synthesis, but pixel-aligned prediction makes the primitive count grow with image resolution and view count, leading to substantial redundancy and increased computational cost. Non-pixel-aligned methods address this limitation by predicting a fixed number of Gaussians. Without explicit ray and depth constraints, these methods yield misplaced Gaussian centers, causing rendering artifacts under large viewpoint changes. To address this issue, we introduce GeoGSplat, a geometry-grounded feed-forward model that explicitly incorporates geometric priors into the non-pixel-aligned framework. A dual-branch encoder constructs Anchors and Triplane to capture coarse 3D structure and fine-grained scene features, respectively. Subsequently, a hierarchical decoder progressively decodes dense 3D points and Gaussian primitives, enabling 3D reconstruction and novel view synthesis. GeoGSplat surpasses both state-of-the-art pixel-aligned and non-pixel-aligned Gaussian Splatting methods on DTU and NRGBD in terms of novel view synthesis and reconstruction. The advantage of our geometry-grounded paradigm further increases as target viewpoints move farther from the input cameras, demonstrating stronger generalization to large viewpoint changes. Project Page:this https URL
  </details>

- **[Efficient Remote Sensing Image-Text Retrieval via Interaction-Free Recall and Text-Guided Reranking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36756)**  `arXiv:2610.36756`  
  _Xi Chen, Xu Chen, Xiamgyang Jia, Wei Wang, Xu Zhang, Zhenyuan Shun_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Remote sensing (RS) image-text retrieval plays a critical role in understanding massive RS imagery. However, the dense multi-object distribution and complex backgrounds in RS imagery make it difficult to simultaneously achieve fine-grained cross-modal alignment and efficient retrieval. Existing methods either rely on complex cross-modal interactions that lead to low retrieval efficiency, or depend on large-scale vision-language adaptation, which requires massive data and computational resources. To address these issues, we develop a fast-then-fine (FTF) retrieval framework centered on interaction-free multi-granular recall and parameter-free text-guided cross-modal reranking with the Balanced Text-guided Interaction Block (BTIB). Specifically, interaction-free multi-granular recall pre-computes gallery embeddings and matches them with the corresponding query embedding without full-gallery token-level text-image interaction, while text-guided cross-modal reranking with BTIB refines local semantic correspondence within the top-K candidate set. Furthermore, an inter- and intra-modal loss is designed to jointly optimize cross-modal alignment across multi-granular representations. Extensive experiments on public benchmarks demonstrate that the FTF achieves competitive retrieval accuracy while significantly improving retrieval efficiency compared with existing methods.
  </details>

- **[Multi-task Disentangling Fusion Mamba-CNN for MODIS Spectral-Temporal Unmixing with Endmember Variability Modeling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36750)**  `arXiv:2610.36750`  
  _Motasem Alkayid, Yimin Zhu, Naser El-Sheimy, Lincoln Linlin Xu_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Spectral-temporal unmixing of MODIS time series is essential for applications such as abundance mapping and spectral library construction, yet it remains challenging due to (i) spectral-temporal information coupling in MODIS data, (ii) intra-class endmember spectral variability and inter-class signature ambiguity, and (iii) the instability of naively fusing multiple expert representations. This paper presents the Multi- task Disentangling Fusion Mamba (MDF-Mamba) to address these limitations. To resolve spectral-temporal coupling, we design a disentangling architecture that pairs per-channel spectral experts with a dedicated bidirectional state-space (Mamba) temporal branch. Convolutional modules each process a single spectral channel, while the Mamba branch encodes the cross-channel seasonal trajectory, forming a multi-branch expert ensemble with soft inter-expert reweighting based on squeeze-and-excitation. For the integration of these spectral and temporal expert outputs, we introduce mHCFusion, adapting the manifold-constrained hyper-connection framework to merge the outputs in a stable and interpretable manner. Endmember variability is addressed by extending the linear mixing model with pixel-wise learnable spectral variability weights that capture intra-class endmember variation. Finally, we design a multi-task training objective that combines supervised abundance estimation with self-supervised spectral reconstruction and endmember regularization terms on the same training data, yielding a highly efficient framework for spectral-temporal unmixing. Experiments on MODIS time series covering the Alberta and Saskatchewan provinces show that MDF-Mamba achieves the best performance on 12 of 14 evaluation metrics across both datasets (7 of 7 on Alberta; 5 of 7 on Saskatchewan), ranking second on the remaining two.
  </details>

- **[Readout and Operating-Point Sensitivity in Event-Stream Candidate Selection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36743)**  `arXiv:2610.36743`  
  _Yifan Shuai_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Event-triggered recording needs a useful combination of target capture and false triggers, yet representation comparisons can change with readout and threshold calibration. We evaluate real event accumulations from 520 DailyDVS-200 clips and 47 participants using participant-disjoint training, validation, and testing. The original study varies frozen encoder scale, accumulation duration, and temporal view count; a post-review extension adds two supervised-pretrained convolutional encoders, a 61,586-parameter trained CNN with three seeds, ten repeated participant partitions, and calibration sensitivity. At 50 ms/eight views under the primary handshake-or-fall policy, DINO-S CLS captures 101/132 targets with 40/388 false triggers, versus 78/132 and 35/388 for patch mean. DINO-B CLS has lower selected-threshold capture but higher fold-wise low-FPR partial AUC (0.746 versus 0.732). MobileNetV3-Small captures 97/132 with 53/388 false triggers, demonstrating why lightweight baselines and actual operating points matter. Across ten dependent repartitions, mean normalized partial AUC is 0.738 for DINO-B CLS and 0.674 for MobileNet. The original LeJEPA S-to-B capture difference is 8.3 points, but its multiplicity-adjusted interval includes zero. Duration and view-count effects remain policy dependent. We find no established JEPA-specific advantage. The evidence concerns offline action-defined candidate selection on previously used development data; it does not establish personal recording value, continuous false alarms per hour, or eyewear energy.
  </details>

- **[Hy-Nomas: Towards Free-form Interactive World Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36729)**  `arXiv:2610.36729`  
  _Qiaoling Chen, Jiaxiang Cheng, Xueqing Deng, Tiankai Hang, Ji Hou, Jiaqi Li, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Interactive world generation requires models to follow successive commands while preserving relevant history and action consequences. We present Hy-Nomas, a video world model with an 8B diffusion backbone that supports free-form text instructions and continuous camera control across multiple interaction turns. It generates variable-length segments conditioned on current inputs and cached history. Our central design aligns structured video captions with action-aware causal attention: descriptive context remains accessible across segments, while action instructions are restricted to their intended temporal windows. A vision-language Prompt Enhancer (PE) translates user requests and multimodal interaction histories into structured conditions for the next segment. A multi-stage curriculum progressively introduces causal generation, text interaction, camera control, and training on model-generated histories, followed by diffusion distillation. In human evaluation, Hy-Nomas shows superior performance over existing world models. We release model weights, code and benchmarks, together with a lightweight adaptation workflow, to support reproducible research on interactive world generation.
  </details>

- **[Progressively Refining SAM for Accurate Phytoplankton Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36722)**  `arXiv:2610.36722`  
  _Jialin Li, Jinkun Jiang, Yuezun Li, Junyu Dong_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Accurate phytoplankton segmentation is essential for ecological monitoring, yet it remains largely underexplored due to the subtle visual characteristics of phytoplankton and the scarcity of pixel-level annotations. To address these challenges, we propose PhySeg, a SAM-based segmentation framework equipped with a lightweight refinement module that progressively restores fine-grained details through multi-scale feature fusion. In addition, we construct a phytoplankton segmentation dataset by manually annotating instance-level masks for an existing dataset, providing a valuable resource for future research. Experimental results demonstrate that PhySeg improves segmentation accuracy, validating the effectiveness of the proposed module. Code is available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[SAGE: Saliency-Annealed Gated Encoder for Universal Vision Recognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36719)**  `arXiv:2610.36719`  
  _Le Yang, Yucheng Huang, Junyu Chen, Qi Dang, Xin Kong, Haifeng Huo, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  When adapting self-supervised Vision Transformers (ViTs) to dense prediction, existing methods often treat the ViT as a passive feature extractor and may underuse the rich priors encoded in its representations, including saliency, global context, and high-frequency details. We propose SAGE (Saliency-Annealed Gated Encoder), an enhanced ViT-Adapter backbone that explicitly exploits these priors through three complementary innovations: (1) a saliency-guided architecture that extracts DINO-style class-token (CLS) saliency to guide feature gating and high-frequency enhancement, together with dynamic multi-layer aggregation and a local-detail branch; (2) an annealing-based training paradigm designed to improve convergence and feature quality during early training while reducing feature drift later in training; and (3) a two-stage, quality-aware training mechanism that smoothly transitions from focal loss to Varifocal Loss (VFL) within a single training run. Across the evaluated tasks (Fig.~\ref{fig:fig_com}), SAGE achieves state-of-the-art (SOTA) or competitive results on COCO (62.8 box AP and 55.3 mask AP), COCO keypoint estimation (78.5 AP), ADE20K (60.3 mIoU for semantic segmentation and 44.9 mask AP for instance segmentation), TinyImageNet classification (94.76\% Top-1 accuracy), and DIOR-R oriented object detection (78.8 AP$_{50}$). The same backbone is used across tasks, with task-specific decoders. To the best of our knowledge, SAGE is the first framework to achieve multi-task state-of-the-art results on Huawei Ascend NPUs. Code and pretrained models will be released after internal review.
  </details>

- **[Look Only When Needed: Certificate-Guided Visual Routing for Efficient Diffusion VLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36671)**  `arXiv:2610.36671`  
  _Donghyeon Cho, Joowon Kim, Seungho Shin, Eunho Yang_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Diffusion vision-language models (DVLMs) process their entire input at every denoising step. Since visual tokens make up 89-97% of this input on the benchmarks we study, most of the decoding computation is spent repeatedly processing the same image. Visual token pruning methods partially relieve this burden by removing part of the visual tokens, but they lose the removed information and still process the remaining image at every step. To measure how often the image is actually needed, we compare LLaDA-V's predictions with and without the image at every decoding step and find that removing the image changes a token being unmasked at only 22.8% of steps on average and at as few as 7.9%. We therefore propose LOWN, a decoding method that decides at every step whether to process the image and how many tokens to unmask. LOWN predicts the masked tokens without the image and processes the image only when this prediction disagrees with the last image-conditioned prediction. Agreement certifies the earlier prediction for reuse, and the number of certified tokens sets how many are unmasked in that step. We further observe that the need for the image differs across benchmarks, so LOWN's adaptive decision is needed rather than a fixed schedule. Without training or a tuned threshold, LOWN retains 99.6% of LLaDA-V's score on ten benchmarks at 35.3% of the decoding FLOPs on average. LOWN also generalizes across LLaDA-based DVLMs, composes orthogonally with visual token pruning, and runs under key-value caching for further efficiency gains. Code and project page are available atthis https URL
  </details>

- **[SAGE: Semantic-Augmented Geometric Experts for Point Cloud Registration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36658)**  `arXiv:2610.36658`  
  _Tomoyasu Shimada, Kazuhiko Murasaki, Shoichiro Takeda, Shogo Sato, Riku Inoue, Toshihiko Nishimura, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Point cloud registration is an essential task for scene reconstruction, requiring the alignment of partially overlapping point clouds. Under low-overlap conditions, this task remains challenging because existing geometry-only methods cannot distinguish geometrically similar but semantically distinct structures (e.g., TV corners vs. desk corners and chair legs vs. table legs), resulting in incorrect correspondences. To address this geometric ambiguity, we present Semantic-Augmented Geometric Experts (SAGE), which integrates frozen pre-trained point cloud semantic representations into the registration pipeline without requiring additional sensors or fine-tuning the semantic encoder. To effectively leverage these semantic distinctions, SAGE employs a Semantic-Guided Mixture of Experts (SG-MoE) architecture. Through semantic-aware routing, SG-MoE enables each expert to specialize in distinct semantic-geometric patterns, reducing mismatches that confound geometry-only methods. With a structure-aware estimator, SAGE achieves the highest registration recall on 3DLoMatch while remaining competitive on 3DMatch. When evaluated with the same number of refinement iterations, SAGE further achieves the highest inlier ratio and registration recall on both benchmarks. These results indicate that frozen semantic representations provide effective cues for resolving geometric ambiguity in low-overlap registration.
  </details>

- **[Adapting Video Models for Robot Control via Motion-guided Asymmetric Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36624)**  `arXiv:2610.36624`  
  _Taeyoung Kim, Junyoung Sung, Heeseung Kwon, Minseop Kim, Jinwoo Shin_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  World Action Models (WAMs) leverage pretrained video generative models for robotic control by jointly predicting future visual observations and actions. The visual prediction objective plays a key role in adapting pretrained video representations to the target robot domain. Yet standard visual prediction inherited from video pretraining treats all visual regions uniformly, despite their differing relevance to robotic control. We therefore investigate the spatial allocation of visual supervision in WAMs and show that focusing supervision on dynamic regions can substantially improve policy performance. Building on this finding, we introduce Motion-Guided Asymmetric Prediction (MoGAP), which augments WAM training with motion-focused visual prediction. MoGAP allocates noise asymmetrically across regions, training the model to reconstruct more heavily corrupted dynamic regions using less corrupted static regions as context. MoGAP is trained alongside standard video-action prediction through a training-only auxiliary prediction path within the shared video diffusion backbone. It uses motion cues derived directly from training videos, requiring neither an external teacher nor additional modalities. Across robot manipulation benchmarks and WAM architectures, MoGAP consistently improves policy performance, increasing success rates on the GR-1 tabletop benchmark from 38.8% to 47.5% for Fast-WAM (Wan2.2-5B) and from 52.3% to 62.3% for Cosmos3-Nano.
  </details>

- **[4D Reconstruction with Events & RGB](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36610)**  `arXiv:2610.36610`  
  _Dulanga Weerakoon, Darshana Rathnayake, Van Minh Do, Hemanth Sabbella, Kaichen Zhou, Fangneng Zhan, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Reconstructing dynamic 3D scenes over time (4D reconstruction) from a single moving camera is central to robotics and AR/VR applications, yet state-of the-art feed-forward methods rely solely on RGB frames and therefore inherit the failure modes of conventional cameras, including motion blur and temporal aliasing under fast or non-uniform camera and object motion. Event cameras report per-pixel brightness changes asynchronously at microsecond resolution, offering a complementary signal that is largely immune to these artifacts, but whether this modality can be incorporated into existing RGB based 4D reconstruction pipelines without a full architectural redesign remains an open question. In this preliminary study, we investigate a minimal extension: we augment Monst3R, an existing RGB-only 4D reconstruction model, with an additional event-camera input branch, leaving the rest of the architecture unchanged. Across 3 benchmark datasets, this simple RGB-event fusion reduces Abs Rel by 3.4% on average relative to the RGB-only Monst3R baseline, and a sensitivity analysis shows that the improvement grows with the magnitude of camera and object motion, with the largest gains observed under high object motion conditions where RGB-only reconstruction degrades most. These early results suggest that even a lightweight, non-invasive fusion of event data can meaningfully improve 4D reconstruction robustness, motivating a more thorough architectural investigation in future work.
  </details>

- **[WithinVAE: Compact Visual Autoencoding with Self-Distilled Semantics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36593)**  `arXiv:2610.36593`  
  _Yuzhou Ji, Jiepeng Wang, Tianyu Jin, Lizhuang Ma, Haibin Huang, Chi Zhang, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Visual autoencoders must balance reconstruction fidelity, compactness, and semantic structure. Existing semantic tokenizers typically inherit semantics from pretrained recognition encoders, whose invariances can discard the appearance detail required for inversion. We show that this inheritance is unnecessary: semantic structure can be learned inside an autoencoder's own reconstructive representation. We introduce WithinVAE, a compact variational visual autoencoder with a sequential bottleneck, DINO/iBOT-style self-distillation from an exponential-moving-average copy of the autoencoder itself, and position-dependent token masking to achieve a 4-fold compression ratio, which is free of pretrained representation encoder or external semantic target. On ImageNet-1K at $256\times256$, WithinVAE improves all four reported reconstruction metrics over RPiAE including PSNR by 1.36 dB, while using half its latent scalar capacity. Under frozen evaluation, the same latents improve linear probing by 6.5 points over RPiAE and 5.1 points over our reconstruction-only baseline, attributing the semantic gains to self-distillation rather than architecture or external supervision. An auxiliary latent-to-latent model reproduces the ordered-compression trend, and a pixel-to-latent model transfers the objective to a pretrained generative space. These results separate the origin of semantic supervision from its use: a compact visual autoencoder can organize semantic structure without borrowing a recognition representation.
  </details>

- **[LabWorld: Learning Interactive 4D Worlds for Scientific Experimentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36592)**  `arXiv:2610.36592`  
  _Suozhi Huang, Kelsey Fu, Binxu Li, Luna Lyu, Kaize Wu, Wenbo Lu, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Autonomous scientific agents must not only reason about experiments but also interact with the physical environments in which they are executed. Existing laboratory simulators typically rely on manually constructed assets and predefined procedures, while laboratory videos capture real equipment, layouts, and interactions but leave this information implicit in pixels. We introduce LabWorld, an agent-driven Real2Sim framework for reconstructing interactive 4D laboratory worlds from monocular RGB video. LabWorld recovers reusable 3D assets, a metric scene, time-varying object states, and procedure events. Multi-frame observations provide complementary evidence for asset and scene reconstruction, while motion reconstruction combines visual and geometric observations with event constraints encoding the spatial and temporal requirements of laboratory interactions. We evaluate LabWorld on real pipetting and vortexing videos collected in our laboratory against general-purpose agentic and dedicated reconstruction baselines. For pipetting, LabWorld achieves a mean Mask IoU of 0.501, exceeding the strongest baseline by 0.033. For vortexing, it achieves a mean Tube-Length-Normalized PCK-AUC of 0.638, exceeding the strongest baseline by 0.035. On five pipetting videos, event-conditioned reconstruction increases the fraction of modeled physical constraints satisfied from 14.3% (5/35) to 100% (35/35). The resulting worlds expose geometry, motion, articulation, and events as explicit, editable state for replay, inspection, and future embodied scientific systems.
  </details>

- **[FrameForge: Harnessing Priors of Video Foundation Models for Unified Image Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36545)**  `arXiv:2610.36545`  
  _Yijing Lin, Zhendong Mao, Mengqi Huang, Yuxin Wang, Yongdong Zhang_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Unified conditional image generation requires a single model to preserve different aspects of a visual input while producing a target image that follows textual guidance. Pretrained video models provide a promising foundation by encoding visual transformations across time, yet existing approaches typically assign the condition and target to fixed temporal positions. We observe that condition-target relationships vary substantially across generation tasks, while video models naturally encode visual transformations at different temporal scales. This motivates us to model conditional generation through input-adaptive virtual temporal relations. We propose FrameForge, which captures two complementary aspects of such relations. Mixture of Temporal Experts (MoTE) models how far the target lies from the condition by dynamically selecting temporal RoPE experts with different virtual intervals. Chain of Temporal Reasoning (CoTR) models how the condition is transformed into the target through a latent intermediate state, with a reasoning-bottleneck attention mask encouraging information propagation through this pathway. Experiments on DreamBench, COCO2017, and GEdit-Bench show that FrameForge achieves superior performance in subject-driven generation, canny-to-image generation, and image editing among the compared methods.
  </details>

- **[Frugal Handwritten Text Recognition for Sub-Saharan Civil-Status Registers: A Smartphone-to-Records Pipeline with CPU-Only Inference](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36540)**  `arXiv:2610.36540`  
  _Ousmane Hamadou, Jean Louis Kedieng Ebongue Fendji_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Handwritten civil-status registers across Sub-Saharan Africa are being photographed, but rarely transcribed, which leaves their content unsearchable. Local administrations that hold these records typically lack GPUs, reliable connectivity, and the budget for external digitization services. We study whether a frugal pipeline, with consumer-grade capture and CPU-only inference, can make such registers machine-readable. We introduce a corpus of 1,568 smartphone images of handwritten birth, marriage, and death records from a municipal registry in Chad, with line-level transcriptions for 185 records. Our pipeline combines a fully classical preprocessing stage, running at about 2.6 images per second on a laptop CPU, with a Kraken recognizer fine-tuned from a pretrained model in about four hours on a free-tier GPU. On manually corrected line segmentation and a single-registry test set whose writers also appear in training, fine-tuning reduces the character error rate from 14.53% to 0.50% and the word error rate from 56.93% to 3.37%. On a six-core laptop CPU, the recognizer weighs 61 MiB, peaks below 2.6 GiB of RAM, and processes an image in under 6 s on average, excluding line segmentation. Finally, a downstream information-extraction task shows that residual recognition errors cost about 9 F1 points on role resolution, compared with a 36.7-point gap between the fine-tuned and pretrained recognizers. These results are evidence of feasibility within one registry, not of performance on unseen writers or sites; we discuss these limitations in detail.
  </details>

- **[Identity or Evidence? Auditing Evidence Dependence in Agentic Life-Science Benchmarks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36510)**  `arXiv:2610.36510`  
  _Danai Brilli, Maria Georgila_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Agentic life-science benchmarks give an agent evidence about a named entity and score an answer against a recorded outcome. That score tests reasoning over evidence only if the evidence, rather than the name, determines the answer. We examine this distinction by varying identity and evidence independently on a clinical-outcome benchmark and published tasks from Biomni and LAB-Bench. Blinding identity matters little when a prompt contains a decisive sequence or passage. It matters a great deal when the prompt is mostly a name and candidate list (0.742 named versus 0.082 blinded; chance 0.093). Across 96 LAB-Bench LitQA2 questions, the gap increases under one fixed passage-redaction scheme (+0.000 to +0.135; per-instance slope +0.147, p= 0.004). On the clinical benchmark, a no-evidence recall probe matches prediction accuracy and recovers a database-specific programme count. So, we argue that benchmarks intended to measure evidence-dependent reasoning should report identity-blinded results, retrieval telemetry, and positive controls.
  </details>
